- `FEATURE-MAP.md`: documented MCP `space_id` requirement on rw tools

**Verification:**
- `cargo nextest run` — 3510/3510 pass, 4 skipped (was 3506; +4 new tests visible since some are smoke-tests gated on `cfg(test)`)
- `npx vitest run` — 9351/9351 pass (no frontend changes; suite stable)
- `prek run --all-files` — all hooks pass

**Process notes:**
- **Coordination disaster.** Three concurrent build subagents (M2, M6, and to a lesser extent C1+C2) ran `git stash` / `git checkout HEAD --` cycles to "clean" the repo before their `cargo nextest` runs, repeatedly wiping each other's uncommitted work. M3+M5 reported having to re-apply its changes 4 times during the session and saved a backup patch at `/tmp/PEND-24-M3-M5.patch`. C1+C2 protected itself by committing 4 WIP commits (`13f8fcd4`…`66f5d4c2`) to `main`. M6 did the same (`e900ab7b`…`c6baf595`). M2 staged its work but landed clean. The orchestrator squashed all 9 WIP commits + uncommitted M3+M5 + uncommitted M2 into a single atomic commit at end-of-session. M4 stalled mid-run (likely deadlocked on a stash/checkout race) and was killed; the orchestrator finished M4 inline as a narrow fix. **Future-session lesson: when launching parallel Rust subagents, hand each one a `git worktree` (one per agent) so their `git checkout HEAD --` resets stay scoped. See PROMPT.md "Worktrees" — the bar is "if subagents touch overlapping directories" but in practice parallel subagents that run cargo tests appear to overlap on `git index` even when their file edits don't.**
- The MCP `space_id` parameter is plain `String`. PEND-18 (`SpaceId` newtype + `SpaceScope` enum) will sweep them up later — already noted in PEND-18's plan body.

**Lessons learned (for future sessions):**
- Subagent prompts MUST forbid `git stash`, `git checkout HEAD --`, `git reset --hard` on files outside the agent's scope. The C1+C2 / M6 strategy of committing WIP commits to protect work is a workaround, not a fix. Worktrees are the structural answer.
- Wiremock's `set_body_string` defaults to `text/plain` content-type; `insert_header` order doesn't reliably override. Use `set_body_raw(bytes, content_type)` for explicit control.
- The `link_metadata` schema migration was deferred (MAINT-213) by choosing the narrowest fix that closes the M4 stated bug. A 1-field schema change can be deferred until the consumer needs it.
- The M6 sibling-case audit (MAINT-214) was the single most useful artefact from the session — it surfaced two unfixed-but-related paths (`restore_all_deleted_inner`, `apply_op_revert`) that a future "page_id sync" pass should clean up.

**Commit plan:** single squashed commit / not pushed.

- **Session 662 (2026-05-04, finished PEND-20 / PEND-25 / PEND-26)** — three-PEND closure batch shipped via 3 parallel build subagents (A=PEND-20 C + PEND-25 L2/L9, B=PEND-26 N3 + PEND-24 H2, C=PEND-20 G frontend) + 1 technical reviewer. **PEND-20 closed:** C — `materializer/handlers.rs` `OpType::PurgeBlock` arm now seeds a `_purge_descendants` TEMP TABLE once via the existing `descendants_cte_purge!()` macro, then 15 downstream DELETE/UPDATE statements read `IN (SELECT id FROM _purge_descendants)` (was 15 CTE re-evaluations). G — `PageBlockStore` (`src/stores/page-blocks.ts`) gained `blocksById: Map<string, FlatBlock>` field + `getBlockById(id)` selector helper; every internal `set` that touches `blocks` now writes a fresh `blocksById` via `buildBlocksById()`; external `store.setState({ blocks })` is wrapped post-creation so partial updates auto-derive the Map (escape hatch: explicit `blocksById` is honored). The `BatchApplyOps` arm wraps in `Arc::new((*record).clone())` because individual records inside `Arc<Vec<OpRecord>>` aren't pre-`Arc`'d (one unavoidable clone, documented inline). L9 — `db.rs::PendingDispatch::Background(Arc<OpRecord>)` and `EditBackground { record: Arc<OpRecord>, ... }`; `enqueue_background` and `enqueue_edit_background` take `impl Into<Arc<OpRecord>>` (not hard `Arc<OpRecord>`) so the ~20 unrelated callsites that move `OpRecord` directly continue to compile via `From<T> for Arc<T>`. The 6 PEND-25-targeted callsites (`commands/properties.rs:163, 433`, `commands/mod.rs:706`, `commands/blocks/crud.rs:507, 687, 820, 1120`) use the explicit `let op_record = Arc::new(op_record); … Arc::clone(&op_record)` pattern to share the `Arc` with downstream `notify_gcal_for_op` and similar consumers. New test `apply_op_arc_record_does_not_leak_strong_count` asserts `Arc::strong_count` returns to 1 after the foreground handler runs. **PEND-26 closed:** N3 + PEND-24 H2 bundled — `recurrence/parser.rs` `shift_date` signature changed from `Option<String>` to `Result<Option<String>, AppError>` (three-channel return: `Ok(Some)` success / `Ok(None)` parse-shape failure stays silent / `Err(AppError::Validation)` for `++` arm dead-ends). `++` arm rewritten with `hit_cap` flag: N3's `let-else` on `shift_date_once` returns `Err(AppError::Validation("recurrence ++ arithmetic overflow: …"))` on `NaiveDate` overflow (was silent `?` propagating `None`); H2's post-loop `if hit_cap { return Err(...) }` returns `Err(AppError::Validation("recurrence ++ cap exceeded: original={original} interval={interval} today={today}"))` instead of stale past-date. `recurrence/compute.rs::handle_recurrence_in_tx` propagates with `?` so the IMMEDIATE tx rolls back. Error variant choice — `AppError::Validation` matches surrounding `recurrence/properties` idiom (15+ existing callsites; the PEND-24 H2 plan example used `InvalidOperation` but the user task said "match surrounding idiom" — `Validation` wins). +4 tests: `plus_plus_with_very_old_origin_returns_err` (H2 cap-exceeded), `plus_plus_overflow_returns_err` (N3 NaiveDate overflow on `2150-01-15 + ++100y`), `plus_plus_boundary_success_under_cap` (success at 6 iterations), `plus_plus_cap_exceeded_propagates_through_handle_recurrence` (caller-level integration, asserts rollback works — zero siblings committed, op_log unchanged). **Process notes:** subagent C's first run was silently reverted by parallel `git stash` operations from sibling subagents A and B (both reported having to stash/unstash to verify in isolation); orchestrator detected the empty file diff post-hoc and relaunched C in the now-stable tree, which shipped clean. - **Session 659 (2026-05-03, PEND-22 frontend robustness fixes)** — single-item S-cost batch closing **PEND-22**: two confirmed gaps from a two-round JS/TS robustness review (75% false-positive validation rate in Round 2). **Item 1 — Graph-worker error envelope:** `src/workers/graph-worker.ts` dispatcher now wraps the `addEventListener('message', …)` body in try/catch that posts a structured `{ type: 'error', message }` to the main thread AND re-throws (preserving the existing boundary `error` event → `onWorkerFailed` fallback path). Plus belt-and-braces global `error` and `unhandledrejection` handlers post the same envelope shape. New `WorkerErrorMessage` interface added to `src/workers/graph-worker-types.ts`; `WorkerOutboundMessage` union extended (now `Tick | Done | Error`). `src/lib/graph-sim-helpers.ts` `handleMessage` extended with a 3rd `else if (msg.type === 'error')` branch that routes through `reportFailure('worker-reported', new Error(msg.message))` and then `worker.terminate()`. The new branch is mutually exclusive with the existing `tick`/`done` branches. **Item 2 — `useQueryExecution` stale-fetch guard:** added `const reqIdRef = useRef(0)` at hook scope; `fetchResults` first statement is `const myReqId = ++reqIdRef.current`; 5 guard sites cover (a) empty-expression early-return only sets error if still current, (b) skip `applyQueryResult` after the `dispatchQuery` await, (c) skip `mergePageTitles` after the `resolvePageTitles` await, (d) skip `setError` in the catch block, (e) skip `endFetch` in the finally block (so loading state stays true until the LATEST fetch settles). Option A (request-id counter) chosen over Option B (cancelled flag in calling effect) because it covers `handleLoadMore` callers without a signature change. **Builder used `satisfies WorkerErrorMessage`** instead of the plan's literal `as WorkerErrorMessage` cast — Biome and tsc both accept; `satisfies` is the modern idiom and doesn't widen the inferred type. **Reviewer PASS, no fixes needed:** confirmed re-throw discipline (boundary `error` event still fires → existing `onWorkerFailed` path intact), no double-report risk (`reportFailure`'s `failed` flag dedups the up-to-three signals a single dispatcher throw could produce: envelope message, global error handler envelope, native boundary error event), all 5 stale-fetch guard sites correctly placed, `reqIdRef` at hook scope (not inside `fetchResults`), entry bump is the FIRST statement, no setState past unmount (implicit because no further fetch will bump the counter), test names accurate ("worker-reported" tag distinct from existing "error" event tag). 1 build subagent + 1 review subagent. **Out-of-scope per plan (intentionally not touched):** `useCheckboxSyntax` optimistic-update ordering (zero-window, not worth the diff), `revertUnclosedMarks` round-trip atomic-token note (lossless mechanism, no fix needed), 30 false positives + 6 exaggerations from the review's first round. **One follow-up flagged by builder (not filed as MAINT):** the "regression-style test that throws inside the `'start'` case of the mock worker" mentioned in the plan's Test impact section was NOT added — would require Node-VM driving a real Worker, disproportionate effort for the worker-side guard's straightforward shape. The structured-error-message test covers the main-thread side of the contract. **REVIEW-LATER.md impact:** open count unchanged (PEND-22 closure deletes the file but adds no MAINT followup since the plan cleanly captures all out-of-scope items). Pending/ folder: 11 → 10 PEND files left. **Tests added:** +2 frontend (worker-reported message routing, stale-fetch result discarding). **Files touched (6, frontend-only):** `src/workers/graph-worker-types.ts` (+6/-1), `src/workers/graph-worker.ts` (+91/-70 — large delta is mostly indentation from try/catch wrap), `src/lib/graph-sim-helpers.ts` (+7), `src/hooks/useQueryExecution.ts` (+14/-4), 2 test files (+27 + +69). Net +219/-70. **Verification:** all 46 prek hooks pass; vitest 9294/9294 (was 9292 + 2 new); tsc 0 errors; biome clean; tauri-mock-parity OK. **Lessons learned:** (1) **`satisfies` over `as`** is the modern type-discipline default — same guarantee, narrower inferred type, harder to drift on rename. (2) **Wrapper-insulated request-id counters (`reqIdRef`)** are strictly better than effect-cleanup-flag patterns for hooks that have multiple call sites (`fetchResults`, `handleLoadMore`) — the counter naturally protects every caller without changing any function signature. (3) **Re-throw-after-postMessage** in worker dispatchers is the right pattern for preserving boundary `error` events — drops nothing, gives the main thread richer signal as a bonus. **Commit plan:** single commit; not pushed.

- **Session 658 (2026-05-03, PEND-03 materializer silent-drop fix)** — single-item M-cost batch closing **PEND-03**: eliminate the silent-drop gap on global cache rebuilds (`RebuildTagsCache`, `RebuildPagesCache`, `RebuildAgendaCache`, `RebuildProjectedAgendaCache`, `RebuildTagInheritanceCache`, `RebuildPageIds`, `RebuildBlockTagRefsCache`) when the materializer's background queue saturates. **Migration 0044 widens `materializer_retry_queue` to global tasks** via the `'__GLOBAL__'` sentinel as `block_id` (SQLite STRICT mode requires PK columns NOT NULL; ULIDs are uppercase Crockford 26-char and cannot collide with the lowercase + underscores 10-char sentinel). The migration uses STRICT — first time PEND-07's policy applies to the materializer surface. Drop+rename pattern ensures no orphan `_new` table on crash. **`RetryKind` enum extended with 7 new global variants** matching `dispatch::FULL_CACHE_REBUILD_TASKS` 1:1; new `is_global()` helper distinguishes per-block vs global tasks for the new `bg_dropped_global` counter. **Consumer + coordinator wired to persist on drop** — the previous silent-drop path in `consumer.rs:533` now calls `record_failure()`; the coordinator's `try_enqueue_background` saturation path also persists. `Materializer` gained a `pub(super) write_pool: SqlitePool` field cloned before move into `run_background` (the `reader_pool` enforces `query_only = ON` and cannot write). Persistence is fire-and-forget via `Self::spawn_task(...)` to keep `try_enqueue_*` synchronous; errors warn but don't propagate. **`bg_dropped_global` counter** added to `QueueMetrics` (separate from `bg_dropped` — operational glance-ability), surfaced through `StatusInfo` to `bindings.ts` (auto-regenerated). **AGENTS.md "Backend Architecture" bullet** added (with explicit user approval): documents the persistence guarantee, the `'__GLOBAL__'` sentinel, the 1h backoff cap as worst-case staleness window, the new `bg_dropped_global` counter, and the explicit list of truly non-retryable tasks (`RebuildFtsIndex`, `FtsOptimize`, `CleanupOrphanedAttachments`, `ReindexFtsReferences`, `RemoveFtsBlock`, `Barrier`, `ApplyOp`, `BatchApplyOps`). **+7 backend tests:** `test_global_task_persistence` (round-trip per-variant for all 7 globals), `test_global_task_backoff` (1m→5m→30m→1h schedule), `test_global_task_dedup` (PK enforces single row per `('__GLOBAL__', kind)`), `test_global_and_per_block_tasks_coexist` (PK separation), `materializer_retry_queue_schema` snapshot (pins STRICT + `task_kind`), `test_global_task_dropped_on_queue_full` (queue-full path persists + bumps counter), `test_global_task_re_enqueued_after_backoff` (sweeper picks up `'__GLOBAL__'` rows). One existing test (`record_failure_ignores_non_retryable_tasks`) updated from `RebuildTagsCache` (now retryable per PEND-03) to `RebuildFtsIndex` (still non-retryable). 1 build subagent + 1 review subagent. **Reviewer PASS:** confirmed migration STRICT-compliance + drop+rename ordering, sentinel discipline (production code goes through the `pub(crate) GLOBAL_TASK_SENTINEL` constant; 4 stray test-only literals noted as minor), RetryKind variants match `FULL_CACHE_REBUILD_TASKS` 1:1, consumer drop-path correctness with no double-counting, coordinator metric wiring, `write_pool` clone-then-move ordering, AGENTS.md placement + phrasing. **Reviewer also filed MAINT-199:** `scripts/check-migrations-strict.mjs` mis-parses `;` inside SQL `--` comments as the statement terminator (real bug, exit 1 on `-- example: id; description` even when STRICT is present). Surfaced during PEND-03 implementation (subagent worked around by stripping inline semicolons from the migration's column comments). Filed with repro, fix sketch (preprocess to strip `--` and `/* */` comments before scanning), cost S, risk Low, impact Medium. **Bonus orchestrator-direct:** `cargo fmt` and end-of-file fixer caught some tiny formatting drift after staging; ran them and re-staged before commit. **REVIEW-LATER.md impact:** open count 21 → 22 (added MAINT-199; reviewer added detail entry but missed the summary-table row + count update, so orchestrator filled both in). Detail entries 23 → 24. Pending/ folder: 12 → 11 PEND files left (plus parallel-authored PEND-19/20/21 added by other sessions). **Files touched (~18):** new migration `0044_materializer_retry_queue_global_tasks.sql` (+44), `materializer/retry_queue.rs` (+455/-67), `coordinator.rs` (+53/-1), `consumer.rs` (+25/-10), `metrics.rs` (+26/-2), `tests.rs` (+103), `AGENTS.md` (+1 bullet), 1 snapshot + ~8 .sqlx renames + `bindings.ts` regen (+51/-37), pending/ housekeeping. **Verification:** all 46 prek hooks pass; backend nextest 3455/3455 (was 3448 + 7 new tests); sqlx prepare --check exit 0; STRICT hook on 0044 exits 0; tsc + biome clean. **Lessons learned:** (1) **Sentinel keys for SQLite STRICT-mode tables** are a clean alternative to nullable PK columns — chose lowercase + underscores so it cannot collide with uppercase Crockford ULIDs. Document the collision-impossibility right next to the constant. (2) **Two-tier counter pattern (`bg_dropped` + `bg_dropped_global`)** beats a single counter with a label dimension — operationally easier to glance at on the status surface. (3) **Reviewers writing to REVIEW-LATER.md need explicit instruction** to update both the summary table AND the count line, not just the detail section — orchestrator caught the gap during the LOG step. (4) **Pre-commit hooks that scan SQL** must strip comments first; the STRICT-hook bug (MAINT-199) is a one-line preprocessor away from being correct. **Commit plan:** single commit; not pushed.

- **Session 657 (2026-05-03, PEND-14 boolean property type)** — single-item M-cost batch closing **PEND-14**: native `boolean` value type added to the property system. **Backend (40 files modified, 2 migrations + 1 snapshot created):** `src-tauri/migrations/0042_add_value_bool_column.sql` adds `value_bool INTEGER` column to `block_properties` with `CHECK (value_bool IS NULL OR value_bool IN (0, 1))` + partial index. `src-tauri/migrations/0043_property_definitions_allow_boolean.sql` recreates `property_definitions` with `'boolean'` added to the value-type CHECK constraint AND uses `STRICT` per PEND-07 (the new policy from session 656). `SetPropertyPayload` in `op.rs` gets `value_bool: Option<bool>` with `#[serde(default)]` (mirrors `DeleteAttachmentPayload.fs_path` for op-log replay back-compat); the validator `validate_set_property` extends to count `value_bool.is_some()`. `create_property_def_inner`'s gating `matches!(...)` includes `"boolean"` (the reviewer-flagged blocker). Dispatch threads through `commands/properties.rs`, `commands/blocks/crud.rs`, `commands/mod.rs`, op-log replay paths in materializer/history/merge/sync_protocol/snapshot/reverse — all `SetPropertyPayload { ... }` literals (86 sites) and `set_property_in_tx`/`set_property_inner` callsites (133 sites) extended with the new field/arg via Python AST script. `.sqlx/` cache regenerated (3 new entries, 3 stale removed). Critical IPC reshape: `setProperty` Tauri command refactored from 6 flat args to bundle-arg `setProperty(blockId, key, value: SetPropertyArgs)` because adding `value_bool` would push the IPC handler to 11 positional args, exceeding specta 2.0.0-rc.24's 10-arg cap. Documented inline. **Frontend (~30 files modified, mostly tests):** `src/lib/tauri.ts:setProperty` wrapper preserves the OUTSIDE shape (`{ blockId, key, valueText?, ... valueBool? }`) so all 12 production callsites stay UNCHANGED — the wrapper internally calls `commands.setProperty(blockId, key, { value_text, value_num, value_date, value_ref, value_bool })`. `tauri-mock/handlers.ts:set_property` reads from the `value` bundle. `PropertyRowEditor.tsx` adds a `valueType === 'boolean'` branch rendering `<Checkbox>`; `AddPropertyPopover.tsx` adds the `'boolean'` `<SelectItem>`. New i18n keys `pageProperty.booleanType` + `property.booleanToggle` in `pages.ts`/`properties.ts`, both in `criticalKeys`. `property-save-utils.ts:buildPropertyParams` handles `'boolean'` save (`'true'/'false'` → `valueBool: true/false`). 4 new PropertyRowEditor tests (3 functional + 1 axe a11y audit) + 1 AddPropertyPopover test asserting the dropdown option. ~30 mock-IPC test assertions reshaped to the new bundle payload shape via Python regex script. **2 build subagents (backend then frontend, sequential due to bindings.ts auto-regen dependency) + 2 review subagents (technical + UX, parallel).** **Tech reviewer caught a real correctness bug:** `reverse/property_ops.rs` dropped `value_bool` when reversing `set_property`/`delete_property` — `PriorPropertyRow` lacked the field, both reverse functions hardcoded `None`, and `find_prior_property` discarded `p.value_bool`. Impact: undoing a boolean property change would emit an empty `SetPropertyPayload` that fails `validate_set_property`. Fixed in 3 surgical edits + 2 regression tests (`reverse_set_property_value_bool`, `reverse_delete_property_restores_value_bool`); confirmed both tests fail without the fix and pass with it. **UX reviewer caught 2 BLOCKING issues per AGENTS.md mandate:** (1) `Checkbox` UI primitive renders at 16/20 px on coarse pointers, well below the 44 px touch-target floor mandated by AGENTS.md "Frontend Development Guidelines / Mandatory patterns / Touch targets". (2) Cell-height mismatch: Checkbox is 16-20 px while adjacent `<Input>` and `<SelectTrigger>` are 28 px (`h-7`), breaking visual coherence in mixed-type property tables. **Fixed orchestrator-direct via local hitbox wrapper** in `PropertyRowEditor.tsx`'s boolean branch: `<div className="flex h-7 items-center [@media(pointer:coarse)]:min-h-11 [@media(pointer:coarse)]:min-w-11 [@media(pointer:coarse)]:justify-center">`. Plus added a 2nd axe a11y audit to `AddPropertyPopover.test.tsx` covering the create-definition surface (the 1st audit doesn't render the boolean dropdown option). **2 follow-up MAINT items filed:** **MAINT-197** (systematic Checkbox primitive fix — augment `ui/checkbox.tsx` with the 44 px hit-area itself, mirroring how Select carries its own touch sizing; after landing, remove the local wrapper) and **MAINT-198** (indeterminate state for `value_bool === null` — reverses the plan's open-question #1 decision, needs explicit user signal). **Deferred per plan (separate future work):** `is_space` retype from `select` to `boolean` (Phase 2 — should land after PEND-18); boolean filtering in `query_by_property`/agenda filters (open-question #4). **REVIEW-LATER impact:** open count 19 → 21 (added MAINT-197 + MAINT-198, no PEND-14 entry since PEND tasks live in pending/ separately). Detail entries 21 → 23. Pending/ folder: 13 → 12 PEND files left. **Tests added:** +9 backend Rust (4 op-log unit + 3 integration + 2 reverse regression), +5 frontend (4 PropertyRowEditor + 1 AddPropertyPopover); +1 frontend a11y audit. **Files touched (~70):** 2 new migrations, 1 new snapshot, 3 new .sqlx queries, 3 stale .sqlx removed, 40 backend files, 1 frontend wrapper, 2 frontend components, 2 i18n files, 28 frontend test files, 1 utility (`property-save-utils.ts`), pending/ housekeeping. **Verification:** all 46 prek hooks pass; backend nextest 3448/3448 pass (was 3446 + 2 new regression tests); frontend vitest 9292/9292 pass (was 9291 + 1 new axe audit); tauri-mock-parity OK (101/101 IPC commands mocked); migrations-strict-tables 0042/0043 pass; tsc 0 errors; biome clean. **Lessons learned:** (1) **`#[serde(default)]` discipline on op-log payload additions is non-negotiable** — without it, every prior op-log entry fails to deserialize on replay. The reviewer's empirical "remove and watch the test fail" verification ritual caught this conclusively. (2) **Tauri command IPC reshape (flat-args → bundle-arg) is a real architectural cost** when specta's 10-arg cap is hit; PEND-14 ate that cost cleanly via the wrapper-insulation strategy (zero production callsite touches, only the wrapper internals + ~30 mock-IPC test assertions changed). (3) **Tech + UX reviewers in parallel pay off** — tech caught a real bug (reverse dropping `value_bool`), UX caught 2 real AGENTS.md violations (touch target + cell height). Neither would have been caught by automated hooks alone. (4) **Local hitbox wrappers are a legitimate stopgap** when a UI primitive lacks the systematic fix; pair with a MAINT- follow-up to land the primitive-level change properly. **Commit plan:** single commit; not pushed.

- **Session 656 (2026-05-03, PEND-07 STRICT tables policy)** — single-item batch closing **PEND-07**: written invariant + pre-commit hook enforcing that every new `CREATE TABLE` in a migration uses SQLite's `STRICT` mode. **AGENTS.md edit landed with explicit user approval** (1 bullet under `## Database` section: "STRICT tables for new schema. Every new CREATE TABLE in a migration must use STRICT. Existing tables are not retrofitted. FTS5 virtual tables don't accept STRICT — they're carved out from the rule. Rationale: SQLite's silent type coercion is a known correctness footgun; STRICT mode (3.37+) catches it at insert time."). New `scripts/check-migrations-strict.mjs` (~69 LOC, pure node stdlib) parses `.sql` files, finds every `CREATE TABLE` (handling `IF NOT EXISTS` and multi-line statements), skips `CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE` (FTS5 carve-out), and asserts the substring after the column-list close-paren contains `\bSTRICT\b` (case-insensitive). Migration-number floor `FIRST_STRICT_MIGRATION = 42` ensures `prek run --all-files` doesn't false-positive on the 41 pre-policy immutable migrations. New `prek.toml` hook entry adjacent to `migrations-immutable` with `pass_filenames = true` and `files = "^src-tauri/migrations/.*\\.sql$"`. **All 5 sanity tests pass on first run:** (1) clean run on existing migrations exits 0; (2) deliberately-bad `CREATE TABLE temp_bad (id INTEGER);` exits 1 with `ERROR: …line 1: CREATE TABLE temp_bad must use STRICT mode (see AGENTS.md § Database)`; (3) STRICT-using migration exits 0; (4) `CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE … USING fts5(...)` exits 0 (carve-out works); (5) `prek run migrations-strict-tables --all-files` exits 0. **Bonus orchestrator-direct fix:** biome rejected the script's `while ((m = re.exec(src)) !== null)` pattern (assignment-in-expression). Refactored to `for (const m of src.matchAll(re))` — the iterator-based pattern is idiomatic for global regex matching in modern JS, has no side-effect-in-condition smell, and produces the exact same match sequence. **REVIEW-LATER.md impact:** open count unchanged (PEND-07 closure deletes the `pending/PEND-07-*.md` file but adds no MAINT followup since the hook caught no real-world violations). Pending/ folder: 13 PEND files left (was 14 last session). **Tests:** +0 (the sanity tests are in the planning doc, not the codebase — the script's own correctness is verified manually via the 5-step ritual). **Files touched (4):** `AGENTS.md` (+1 bullet under § Database), `scripts/check-migrations-strict.mjs` (new, +69 LOC; orchestrator post-edit -1/+1 = 0 net for the matchAll refactor), `prek.toml` (+17 LOC for new hook block), `pending/PEND-07-*.md` (deleted, -83 LOC). **Verification:** all 45 prek hooks pass (the new `migrations-strict-tables` hook is the 46th, so it's now 46 hooks). **Lessons:** (1) PROMPT.md's "AGENTS.md updates need explicit user approval" is rigid and worth respecting — the user approved PEND-07's AGENTS.md edit before the batch ran, removing the gate. (2) The `pass_filenames = true` + migration-number-floor combination keeps the hook fast (only changed files in normal use) without false-positives during `--all-files` audits — better than baseline-allowlists which need maintenance. (3) Modern JS `matchAll(re)` iterator beats the legacy `while ((m = re.exec) !== null)` pattern — biome flags the latter, and matchAll is more idiomatic anyway. **Commit plan:** single commit; not pushed.

- **Session 655 (2026-05-03, Batch ARCH-FOLLOWUP-2: PEND-13 page_id ↔ space drift audit)** — single-item batch closing **PEND-13**: integration test asserting three invariants on every live block (`is_conflict = 0 AND deleted_at IS NULL`) — (A) `page_id` denormalization correctness, (B) space-property existence (exactly one for non-space pages; zero for space blocks), (C) transitive consistency between `space_via_page_id` and `space_via_ancestor_chain`. Two test variants landed in `src-tauri/src/integration_tests.rs` (+441 LOC, test-only, no production-code changes): `page_id_space_drift_audit_per_block` (fixture: 2 spaces + 2 user pages + 3 content blocks + cross-space move via `move_block_inner`) and `page_id_space_drift_audit_after_lifecycle_ops` (same base + audit after each of `DeleteBlock` → `RestoreBlock` → `PurgeBlock` → `SetProperty(space=...)`). 3 file-local helpers (`compute_page_id_via_cte`, `resolve_space_via_page_id`, `resolve_space_via_ancestor_chain`) plus a shared `run_drift_audit(pool, label)` use `sqlx::query_scalar!` macros so the .sqlx cache catches future SQL drift. **Both tests pass on first run** — no drift surfaced, which confirms the `page_id` rebuild + space property invariants currently hold (unlike PEND-05 last session, which DID surface drift on `.+1w` and was `#[ignore]`d as MAINT-196). 1 build subagent (PEND-13 implementation) + 1 review subagent (technical review). **Reviewer audit confirmed:** (1) `compute_page_id_via_cte` is a faithful semantic mirror of production's `cache/page_id.rs::rebuild_page_ids_impl` (same seed shape, recursive-member join structure, exit predicates, terminal projection); (2) every recursive CTE filters `is_conflict = 0` in BOTH seed AND recursive members AND bounds `depth < 100` per AGENTS.md invariant #9; (3) lifecycle ops correctly handled — soft-deleted blocks skipped via `deleted_at IS NULL`, purged blocks absent from `blocks`, `SetProperty(space=...)` resolves through both paths; (4) page convention `page_id = self.id` (per `commands/blocks/crud.rs:210-211`) handled correctly by the CTE oracle (recursion stops at depth 0 when seed is a page). **Reviewer noted two minor follow-ups (not blockers):** (a) fixture lacks an explicit conflict-copy row that would behaviourally exercise the `is_conflict = 0` filter (the plan's open-question #2 deferred this; recommend adding a raw-SQL conflict copy with bogus `page_id` later); (b) `run_drift_audit` uses runtime `sqlx::query_as` for the top-level `SELECT id, block_type, page_id FROM blocks WHERE …` instead of the macro variant — small loss of compile-time drift detection on a trivial query. Neither warranted a fix in this batch. **Bonus orchestrator-direct cleanup:** `cargo fmt` flagged 5 lines in the new test that didn't match rustfmt style; orchestrator ran `cargo fmt` (1 insertion / 4 deletions) before re-running prek. **REVIEW-LATER impact:** open count unchanged (PEND-13 is a PEND task, not a REVIEW-LATER item; closure deletes the `pending/PEND-13-*.md` file but adds no MAINT followup since the test passed on first run). Pending/ folder: 14 PEND files left (was 15 last session). **Tests added:** +2 backend Rust integration tests + 4 file-local helpers. **Files touched (3):** `src-tauri/src/integration_tests.rs` (+441 LOC then +1/-4 fmt), `pending/README.md` (status update + recommended-order reorder), `pending/PEND-13-*.md` (deleted), plus 8 new `.sqlx/` cache entries (one belongs to a previously-uncached `sync_protocol/tests.rs:1193` query that `cargo sqlx prepare --tests` picked up incidentally — benign, cache stays consistent). **Verification:** all 45 prek hooks pass; `cargo nextest run -E 'test(page_id_space_drift_audit)'` 2 pass; full nextest sanity 3439/3439 pass; `cargo sqlx prepare --check -- --tests` exit 0. **Lessons:** (1) "expected-to-pass" tests differ procedurally from "expected-to-discover-drift" tests — PEND-13's prompt explicitly distinguished the two cases (pass on first run = ship; fail on first run = `#[ignore]` + file MAINT, mirror PEND-05/MAINT-196 pattern). The subagent followed this cleanly, no stall. (2) `sqlx::query_scalar!` macros for test helpers cost nothing extra and turn future SQL drift into compile errors instead of runtime test failures. (3) Splitting a fixture into `(test 1: cross-space-move) + (test 2: lifecycle ops)` is more discoverable than a single mega-test — failures point directly at which op type drifted. **Commit plan:** single commit; not pushed.

(2) **MAINT-185 revert** (commit e8b0ac2): the pre-push playwright hook caught 57/305 e2e failures (the per-batch prek hook doesn't include playwright, so this run had been silently regressing). Bisect across the 99 commits ahead of origin/main isolated **e76cf68 (Batch FE-MAINT-CLUSTER-2 / MAINT-185 `useBlockKeyboard` ref-bag refactor)** as the regression. Root cause: MAINT-185 reduced `useCallback` deps from 16 (editor + 15 callbacks) to 1 (just `editor`), but the keydown listener attaches to `editor.view.dom.parentElement` — a DOM reference that can change identity between renders when `EditorContent` unmounts/remounts (e.g., on focus shift via "Add block"). Under the old dep-heavy pattern, every callback identity change re-fired the `useEffect`, which re-grabbed a fresh `parentElement`. MAINT-185 broke that masking; the listener stayed bound to a stale parent. Reverted both `use-block-keyboard.ts` AND the 2 MAINT-185 unit tests (`refs-bag stability`) that pinned the broken pattern. Filed **MAINT-194** with three correct-fix paths (memoize callbacks at call site, watch parentElement explicitly, or document-level listener with isFocused dispatch). (3) **e2e i18n drift** (also commit e8b0ac2): with MAINT-185 reverted, 2 e2e tests still failed because they asserted pre-rename i18n strings: `'Search blocks...'` → `'Search blocks (3+ chars)...'` (changed in `56baeaa`/UX-TRIVIAL-5) and `'Code'` (toolbar.code) → `'Inline code'`. (4) **MAINT-195 filed**: discovered a stale pre-existing WIP in the working tree containing a 4-line `BlockNotes → Agaric` rename of the mDNS service name constant — leftover from project rename. Stashed as `wip-mdns-blocknotes-to-agaric-rename`. Filed as MAINT-195 with verdict that it's safe to land (announce TYPE `_agaric._tcp.local.` is the discovery key; NAME prefix is just a within-type label, no protocol break). (5) **Push gate identified** — `prek.toml:14` wires `no-commit-to-branch --branch=main` as a `pre-push` stage hook. The 99 commits stacked on local main from this multi-session run cannot be pushed via `git push origin main` without `--no-verify`. User chose "Don't push yet" — commits remain local for review. **Methodology lesson — pre-push hooks are not pre-commit hooks.** The 13 batches in this run all PASSED prek-on-commit (which excludes `stages = ["pre-push"]` hooks: full playwright + sqlx prepare --check). MAINT-185's e2e regression was therefore silent for the entire run. Remediation: future "extended verification" rounds should explicitly run `npx playwright test` even outside pre-push, and ideally surface a single per-session checklist (clippy --all-targets, full playwright, full vitest, full nextest) at run-end before the user expects to push. Final state: working tree CLEAN (only the mDNS WIP stashed); 100 commits ahead of origin/main on local main; 305/305 e2e pass; 9284/9284 vitest pass; 3437/3437 nextest pass; 44 prek hooks pass; 0 clippy warnings (lib OR all-targets); all builds OK.

Property-CRUD / formatting-action-handler files (`BlockPropertyEditor`) tend to have meaningful arg signatures (block-id, property-key, value, placement, search-string) → real violations. Hook / chain-API / lifecycle-callback files (`useBlockKeyboardHandlers`, `HeadingLevelSelector`, `BlockContextMenu.onClose`) tend to have zero-arg signatures → no real violations. **The right strategy is to launch parallel audit subagents on the next 4 un-audited files** (`FormattingToolbar` / `GraphView` / `useUndoShortcuts` / `UnlinkedReferences`) — each will independently produce either tightenings or annotations, and the orchestrator's per-file ROI is positive in both cases (the entry's per-file row gets resolved either way). - **Session 648 (2026-05-03)** — Batch MIX-5 closed (1 item via orchestrator-direct, **eleventh back-to-back FE-cleanup-style batch**, retry of L-17 after MIX-4 subagent stall): **L-17** (`dispatch_op` enqueues fg+bg out of order). The fix was a **3-line behavioral change** in `src-tauri/src/materializer/dispatch.rs:128-147` — between `enqueue_foreground(ApplyOp(...))` and `enqueue_background_tasks(...)`, insert `self.flush_foreground().await?`. `flush_foreground` already exists (`coordinator.rs:675-681`): it appends a `MaterializeTask::Barrier(Notify)` and `await`s the consumer's `notify_one`. Because the fg consumer is FIFO, the Barrier is processed AFTER the ApplyOp commits — so by the time `flush_foreground` returns, ApplyOp has drained and `blocks` is fully committed. Only THEN does the bg fan-out get enqueued, eliminating the race where a bg consumer could pull `RebuildTagsCache` and run it against pre-`CreateBlock` state. Doc-comment expanded to cite L-17 and explain the gating. Full Rust suite passes (3437/3437) — only one test required updating: `materializer::tests::concurrent_dispatch` at L3212 was a strict-equality `fg_processed == 11` (10 ApplyOps + 1 final flush Barrier). After the fix, every `dispatch_op` enqueues an additional internal Barrier between ApplyOp and bg, so 10 dispatch_ops emit 10 ApplyOps + 10 dispatch barriers + 1 final flush = 21. Updated to `21` with a comment citing L-17 explaining the new accounting. **Why orchestrator-direct succeeded:** in retrospect this was always a 3-LOC behavioral fix, not the architectural refactor the L-17 entry's "Recommendation" section described (option (a): "move the bg fan-out *into* the fg consumer so it runs only after `apply_op_tx` commits"). The simpler insight — that a Barrier-gated `flush_foreground` already provides the exact ordering guarantee — only emerged once orchestrator read `coordinator.rs:flush_foreground` and saw the Notify+Barrier primitive. The MIX-4 subagent (and the L-17 author at filing time) were both anchored on the bigger refactor framing. **Methodology lesson:** When a "Recommendation" in REVIEW-LATER points at a multi-file refactor but the underlying invariant is achievable via a single existing primitive, the orchestrator's whole-codebase context wins over a subagent's narrower file-scope reading. This is the second time in the run a stalled subagent was unblocked by orchestrator-direct (FE-M-8 in s643 after stalling in s641). The pattern: **after a subagent stall, do NOT immediately re-spawn — first re-read the surrounding primitive set** for an existing-tool fix that the subagent may have missed. The pinning test renamed `export_page_markdown_inner_with_malformed_id_returns_not_found` → `..._returns_ulid_error`, asserting `Err(AppError::Ulid(_))`. **Ripple effect:** 3 other tests (`export_page_markdown_walks_full_subtree_with_pagination`, `export_page_markdown_batch_resolves_mixed_references`, `export_page_markdown_handles_many_unrelated_tags`) used fake-ULID page IDs containing `'O'`/`'I'`/`'L'` (e.g. `01EXPORTPAGE0000000000PAGE`) which were silently accepted before but now reject — Crockford base32 excludes `I, L, O, U`. Replaced with valid alternatives: `01EXP0RTPAGE...` (O→0), `01EXP0RTPAGEM1XED...` (O→0, I→1), `01EXP0RTSPARSE...` (O→0), `01C0NTENTBKK...` (O→0, L→K). The internal child-block IDs containing `'L'` (e.g. `01AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAABLK1`) were left alone — they're never validated by `BlockId::from_string` because they're not the top-level `page_id` arg, only stored/queried as strings via `WHERE id = ?` in the descendant walk. Test count unchanged: 37/37 page tests pass. **Methodology note:** orchestrator-direct fixes work well for genuinely tiny edits (1-15 LOC) but the L-136 ripple shows that even "trivial" Rust changes can have ripple-effect costs — 3 separate tests had to be patched because they encoded fake-ULID assumptions that the new validation rejects. The test file required a per-file `vi.mock('@/components/ui/select', …)` override because the shared mock at `src/__tests__/mocks/ui-select.tsx` renders SelectTrigger as `null` and drops children — the override forwards children into a sibling `<div data-slot="select-trigger-children">` while preserving the prop-capture chain the 9 pre-existing tests rely on. **UX-368** replaces the simple-string SpaceSwitcher tooltip with a stacked layout listing the first 5 digit mappings ("Ctrl+1 Personal · Ctrl+2 Work · …"). `availableSpaces` and `spaceHotkeyHint` were already in scope (used in the dropdown rows); no new imports. **UX-378** adds inline `host:port` validation to PeerListItem's address Input via `useMemo`-derived `addressError`: returns `null` (empty), `'format'` (regex `/^[\w.-]+:\d{1,5}$/` fails), or `'port'` (port out of 1-65535). Inline `<p role="alert">` shows the matching i18n message; Input gets `aria-invalid` + `aria-describedby`; Save button `disabled` extended with `|| addressError != null`. Two new i18n keys (`device.addressFormatInvalid`, `device.addressPortInvalid`). The existing `handleSaveAddress` toast-on-failure preserved as defense-in-depth. One pre-existing test that typed an invalid address to trigger the server-rejection toast updated to use a syntactically-valid one — same pattern as the UX-339 test-fix cascade in session 629. **UX-394** rewrites `findConflicts` in `keyboard-config/storage.ts` to respect the `condition` field. Two-pass implementation: Pass 1 groups by `(keys, category, condition ?? '__wildcard__')` — exact-triple duplicates incl. wildcard×wildcard. Pass 2 cross-pairs every wildcard with every conditioned shortcut on the same `(keys, category)` — wildcards collide with everything. Two shortcuts with **different defined conditions** never conflict (disjoint by construction). The previous test that documented the false-positive is rewritten to assert the correct new behavior; the `prevBlock`/`nextBlock` rebind test was reframed because those defaults have different defined conditions (`atStart`/`atEnd`) so rebinding to the same keys does NOT conflict — replaced with a `saveBlock`/`indentBlock` (both unconditional) variant that preserves the original test's intent. 4 new tests cover wildcard-cross and different-conditions cases. **Orchestrator catch:** the prek `tsc --noEmit` hook caught a strict-types issue the subagent's local tsc missed: the new UX-325 test passed `value_ref: undefined` explicitly to a `Partial<{ ... value_ref: string }>` argument, which is rejected under `exactOptionalPropertyTypes: true`. Fixed by omitting the property entirely. ## Session 621 — Batch QUICK-WINS-MIX-2: 4 quick wins parallelized (2026-05-02)

| Metadata | Value |
|----------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-02 |
| **Subagents** | 4 build subagents (parallel) + 1 combined review subagent |
| **Items closed** | MAINT-114, MAINT-180, MAINT-189 (3 fully closed); MAINT-192 partial (UX.md (a)+(b) landed; AGENTS.md (c)+(d) gated) |
| **Items dropped from Quick wins listing** | PERF-19, PERF-20 (their detail blocks already say "Defer — keep tracked in REVIEW-LATER as a deliberate non-fix"; they were stale on the Quick-wins ready-to-grab list) |
| **Tests added** | +10 frontend (8 new `usePropertyKeysCache.test.ts` tests + 2 new `SpaceManageDialog` IPC-dedup contract tests). 0 backend (the 4 items are all frontend / docs / DevOps) |
| **Files touched** | 14 source + 2 docs (UX.md, AGENTS.md UNTOUCHED) + 1 zizmor baseline + REVIEW-LATER + SESSION-LOG |

**Summary:** Closed 4 quick wins in parallel via 4 build subagents (parallelism per PROMPT.md "Target 5-6 concurrent subagents"). Items spanned 4 different domains (DevOps workflow, frontend dialog hardening, frontend hook extraction, docs) so the parallelism was honest — non-overlapping files, no merge friction.

**MAINT-114** (DevOps): Folded `release-tag.yml` (78 LOC, `workflow_dispatch`-only, ran `bump-version.sh`) into `release.yml` as a `workflow_dispatch`-gated `bump-version` job sitting above `verify-version`. Net file count: 4 → 3. Net LOC: 542 → 524 (-18; the abandon-guard "merged file must be net shorter than the sum of originals" was satisfied so the spike landed). Job graph: dispatch → bump-version (gated `if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'`) → verify-version → validate → build matrix; tag-push flow → bump-version skipped → relies on GH Actions "skipped predecessors are treated as success for `needs:`" semantics (verified via official docs). zizmor baseline rebaselined: removed all `release-tag.yml:*` entries (file gone), shifted `release.yml:N` line numbers (+60 for content after `jobs:`, +10 for `permissions:`), added 4 entries for the new `bump-version` job; the 6 prior `template-injection` entries on `release-tag.yml` were dropped because the new bump-version uses `env: INPUT_VERSION:` indirection rather than direct `${{ inputs.version }}` interpolation in `run:` blocks (zizmor's rule doesn't fire on the safer pattern). Side effect: zizmor baseline is mildly cleaner; MAINT-193 baseline triage remains deferred.

**MAINT-180** (Frontend): Lifted the per-`SpaceRowEditor` IPC probes out of the row component into the parent `SpaceManageDialog`. Two probe types: emptiness (`listBlocks({ blockType: 'page', spaceId, limit: 1 })`) and journal-template (`getProperties(spaceId)` — note: REVIEW-LATER's spec said `listBlocks` for both probes, but the source uses `getProperties` for the template; the subagent caught and corrected this). Parent now owns two `Record<string, T>` state maps keyed by `space.id` plus two `useRef<Set<string>>` to track in-flight fetches; rows take three new props (`emptiness`, `initialJournalTemplate`, `onJournalTemplateCommitted`). The per-row `cancelled` flag pattern was removed entirely — without per-row IPCs, the parent's state setter is the single sink and React 18+ tolerates set-after-unmount (cf. UX.md Common Pitfall #39 just landed in MAINT-192 (a)). IPC volume: 2×N×M → 2×N (re-opens with the same `space.id` set are zero-IPC cache hits). 2 new "MAINT-180 — IPC dedup contract" tests pin (a) "1 IPC per `space.id` on initial open, not 1-per-row-mount" and (b) "close + reopen with same set is no-op".

**MAINT-189** (Frontend): Extracted shared `usePropertyKeysCache` hook so `PropertyValuePicker`, `UnlinkedReferences`, `LinkedReferences` all share one `listPropertyKeys()` result per active `spaceId` instead of fetching per-mount. Implementation: module-level `Map<spaceId, string[]>` + lazy Tauri `block:properties-changed` invalidation listener, exposed via `useSyncExternalStore` for the React subscription (matches the codebase's existing convention; not a new Zustand store, per AGENTS.md "Architectural Stability"). 8 new hook tests pin: (a) two consecutive mounts fire 1 IPC; (b) different spaceIds are separate slots; (c) `block:properties-changed` event clears the cache; (d) third consumer after invalidation refetches; plus cache-hit-on-remount, null-spaceId-is-its-own-slot, programmatic invalidate, error-path-falls-back-to-empty. IPC volume: N consumer mounts × M views → 1 IPC per `spaceId` (verified by an ad-hoc test that mounted the hook 6 times and observed exactly 1 IPC, then deleted the ad-hoc file). Behavior change to flag: `LinkedReferences`-only `toast.error('references.loadPropertiesFailed')` on `listPropertyKeys` failure was replaced with a uniform `logger.warn` (matching the other two consumers, which never toasted). 2 existing `LinkedReferences` tests updated to reflect the new uniform behavior. The subagent argued this is intentional consolidation and offered a 4-line rollback if the parent agent wanted the toast back; reviewer agreed the consolidation is the right call.

**MAINT-192 partial** (Docs): Added two UX.md entries that the post-MAINT-173 review pass would have found (its 74% false-positive rate traced to React-class anti-patterns that don't apply here):
- **Common Pitfall #39** — `setState` after unmount in React 18+ is no longer a defect; React 18 silently drops the late update; only flag when the dropped update would leave incorrect *visible* state.
- **Lessons Learned → Data & State** — `useStore.getState()` inside callbacks is intentional; reads the latest state from the Zustand store (not the closure); cited call sites: `useSyncTrigger.ts:189` (online-event handler reading sync state) and `useAppKeyboardShortcuts.ts:135` (tab-shortcut callback reading current space).

The (c) + (d) AGENTS.md additions in MAINT-192's spec — picker debouncing convention (referencing `useDebouncedCallback` + 300 ms used by `TagFilterPanel`) and `INTERNAL_PROPERTY_KEYS` reference under the "Properties system is the primary extension point" section — are gated on the AGENTS.md self-rule "No changes to this file (AGENTS.md) without explicit user approval. Ever." and remain open. MAINT-192 stays in REVIEW-LATER with its summary row + detail rewritten to scope only those two remaining sub-items.

**REVIEW-LATER impact:**
- **Top-level open count:** 114 → 111 (-3 closed). MAINT-114 row + detail removed; MAINT-180 row + detail removed; MAINT-189 row + detail removed. MAINT-192 row + detail rewritten in-place to scope only (c) + (d).
- **Detail entries:** 149 → 146 (-3 detail blocks).
- **Quick wins subsection:** 6 entries → 3 entries. Removed PERF-19, PERF-20 (deferred-non-fixes that should never have been listed as "ready to grab"), MAINT-114 (now closed). MAINT-192 entry rewritten to flag the AGENTS.md gate.
- **Previously resolved counter:** 947+ → 950+ across 620 → 621 sessions.

**Verification:**
- `cd src-tauri && cargo nextest run` — 3433 tests run, 3433 passed (3 skipped). No backend changes; the run validates that nothing regressed.
- `npx vitest run` — 9088 frontend tests pass (+10 from Session 620: +8 hook + 2 dedup contract).
- `npx tsc --noEmit` — 0 errors.
- `prek run --all-files` — pending until commit.

**Process notes:**
- **4 parallel subagents on non-overlapping file boundaries worked cleanly.** Files touched per subagent: A (3 .yml files), B (2 frontend files), C (8 frontend files including 1 new hook + 1 new test), D (1 docs file). No merge conflicts; no shared file edits across subagents. PROMPT.md's parallelism guidance (target 5-6 concurrent subagents) paid off — 4 items closed in roughly the time of the slowest single item.
- **Spec discrepancy caught by subagent.** MAINT-180's REVIEW-LATER detail said both probes were `listBlocks` calls; the actual journal-template fetch is `getProperties`. Subagent caught the discrepancy, corrected the lift, and flagged it in the report. This is the kind of finding worth surfacing.
- **PERF-19 / PERF-20 stale Quick-wins listing.** Their summary-table rows are kept (their detail says "track in REVIEW-LATER as a deliberate non-fix") but the Quick-wins listing was incorrect; removed from that listing. Worth noting that the per-section-detail "Decision: Defer" should always trump the Quick-wins listing.
- **AGENTS.md gating discipline.** 4 of the 4 build subagents respected the AGENTS.md self-rule; the MAINT-192 subagent explicitly noted the (c) + (d) deferral and asked for user approval. This is the correct pattern.
- **Behavior changes flagged for review.** MAINT-189's lost `LinkedReferences` toast was the only user-visible regression introduced this session. Flagged in the subagent report + this session log + the technical reviewer's findings, all of which agreed the consolidation is justified (only 1/3 consumers had the toast; uniformity > one-off). If the user disagrees, the rollback is 4 lines on the hook (return `{ keys, error }` instead of `keys`).

**Lessons learned (for future sessions):**
- **PROMPT.md Quick-wins as a planning aid is only as good as its detail entries.** When a "ready to grab" item turns out to be a documented non-fix (PERF-19 / PERF-20), the listing has lied. Future sessions should re-check the per-section detail before acting on the Quick-wins listing.
- **Spec discrepancies (MAINT-180's `listBlocks` vs `getProperties`) should be normalized into the REVIEW-LATER detail at file time.** When a spec describes the wrong IPC, the migration costs 5 minutes more than it should. Authors of new REVIEW-LATER entries should verify IPC names against the source.
- **The `useSyncExternalStore` + module-level cache pattern is the right shape for shared frontend caches.** Doesn't introduce a new Zustand store (AGENTS.md "Architectural Stability" warns against new stores), preserves React's tearing-safety, and the lazy Tauri-event listener avoids `useEffect` lifecycle issues. Worth promoting as a convention if more shared caches land.

**Commit plan:** single commit, not pushed.

## Session 620 — MAINT-113 M2 closed: backlink + tag-query helpers retyped (2026-05-02)

| Metadata | Value |
|----------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-02 |
| **Subagents** | orchestrator-only build + 1 explore-profile reviewer (PASS, all categories) |
| **Items closed** | — (MAINT-113 M2 closed; MAINT-113 remains open for M3) |
| **Items modified** | MAINT-113 (M2 progress recorded in summary + detail; M3 plan refined with the explicit dispatcher decision + `commands/properties.rs` `set_*_inner` retype path) |
| **Tests added** | — (the 3 `.into()` widenings on `String` collection targets don't change coverage) |
| **Files touched** | 9 source + 1 new `.sqlx/` cache entry + REVIEW-LATER + SESSION-LOG |

**Summary:** Closed MAINT-113 M2. Retyped the backlink and tag-query helper cluster — a non-dispatcher, non-trash-adjacent set of helpers that could migrate cleanly without forcing the polymorphic `list_blocks_inner` decision. `BacklinkQueryResponse.items` and `BacklinkGroup.blocks` now type their rows as `ActiveBlockRow`. The three backlink resolvers (`eval_backlink_query`, `eval_backlink_query_grouped`, `eval_unlinked_references`) use the M1.5-introduced `ActiveBlockRow::from_block_row_unchecked` boundary cast over their already-active-filtered ID sets. `eval_tag_query` switches its `query_as` type parameter from `BlockRow` to `ActiveBlockRow` directly (sqlx's `FromRow` derive handles the typed-id slot via `sqlx::Type` transparent over `String`). `pagination::list_backlinks` adopts the sqlx column-cast hint `id as "id: crate::ulid::ActiveBlockId"` in its `query_as!` macro — exercising the third recipe for typed-id retypes (alongside the M1.5 boundary-cast and M2 query_as-typed patterns). The two affected Tauri commands (`get_backlinks`, `query_by_tags`) propagate the typed return.

**Design rationale:** The `list_blocks_inner` polymorphic dispatcher was deliberately deferred to M3. It fans into `list_children` / `list_by_type` / `list_by_tag` / `list_agenda*` (active) **and** `list_trash` (deleted blocks) into a single `Result<PageResponse<BlockRow>, AppError>` return type. Retyping any one branch forces the others to align. Two paths exist (split `list_trash` into a dedicated Tauri command vs. narrow at the call site via downcast) and AGENTS.md "Architectural Stability" requires explicit user approval for the split-IPC path. The backlink + tag-query cluster sits outside this dispatcher and therefore migrated without architectural friction. `commands/properties.rs::set_*_inner` family (9 functions taking `block_id: String` and returning `BlockRow`) was also deferred to M3 because tightening the type-safety win at those sites requires `verify_active` IPC gates — a different shape of change than the row-type retype.

**REVIEW-LATER impact:**
- **Top-level open count:** 114 → 114 (no closure; MAINT-113 remains open with M2 closed and M3 next).
- **Detail entries:** 149 → 149 (MAINT-113's milestone block + summary row rewritten in place).
- **Previously resolved:** 947+ (unchanged).

**Files touched (this session):**
- `src-tauri/src/backlink/types.rs` (+8 LOC; `BacklinkQueryResponse.items` + `BacklinkGroup.blocks` retyped to `Vec<ActiveBlockRow>` + M2 doc comments)
- `src-tauri/src/backlink/query.rs` (+10/-5 LOC; boundary cast at `eval_backlink_query`'s row-fetch step; cursor encoding uses `last.id.as_str().to_string()`)
- `src-tauri/src/backlink/grouped.rs` (+12/-4 LOC; boundary casts at the per-group block-row construction in both `eval_backlink_query_grouped` and `eval_unlinked_references`)
- `src-tauri/src/backlink/tests.rs` (2 sites: `b.id.clone().into()` for `FxHashSet<String>` collection)
- `src-tauri/src/tag_query/query.rs` (return type + `query_as::<_, ActiveBlockRow>` swap + cursor encoding adjusted; M2 doc comment on the sqlx::Type transparent mechanism; import update)
- `src-tauri/src/pagination/links.rs` (return type; sqlx column-cast hint `id as "id: crate::ulid::ActiveBlockId"` in the `query_as!` macro; cursor encoding adjusted; M2 doc comment; import update)
- `src-tauri/src/commands/queries.rs` (`get_backlinks_inner` + `get_backlinks` Tauri command return-type propagation)
- `src-tauri/src/commands/tags.rs` (`query_by_tags_inner` + `query_by_tags` Tauri command return-type propagation; import update)
- `src-tauri/src/commands/tests/query_cmd_tests.rs` (2 sites: `b.id.clone().into()` widenings for `HashSet<String>` collection in the grouped-backlinks tests)
- `src-tauri/src/command_integration_tests/backlink_integration.rs` (1 site: `b.id.clone().into()` for `HashSet<String>` collection)
- `src-tauri/.sqlx/query-*.json` (1 new cache entry for `list_backlinks`'s typed-id query)
- `src/lib/bindings.ts` (regenerated: `BacklinkQueryResponse.items` + `BacklinkGroup.blocks` typed `ActiveBlockRow[]`; `getBacklinks` and `queryByTags` return types updated)
- `REVIEW-LATER.md` (MAINT-113 entry — summary row + detail block rewritten with M2 closed, M3 plan refined)
- `SESSION-LOG.md` (this entry + Recent milestones bump)

**Verification:**
- `cd src-tauri && cargo nextest run` — 3433 tests run, 3433 passed (3 skipped).
- `npx vitest run` — 9078 frontend tests pass.
- `cargo sqlx prepare -- --tests` — 1 new `.sqlx/` cache entry committed for the typed-id `list_backlinks` query.
- `cargo test -- specta_tests --ignored` — bindings regenerated; `ts_bindings_up_to_date` passes.
- `npx tsc --noEmit` — 0 errors (frontend types continue to accept the new bindings without changes via TS structural typing).
- `prek run --all-files` — pending until commit.

**Process notes:**
- **Three boundary-cast recipes are now in active use:** (1) M1's `ActiveBlockId::from_trusted_active(&str)` for direct ID construction, (2) M1.5's `ActiveBlockRow::from_block_row_unchecked(BlockRow)` for retrofitting an existing raw row at a known-active boundary (`fts::search_fts`, `gcal_push::connector`'s reverse direction, M2's three backlink resolvers), and (3) M2's `query_as!(ActiveBlockRow, "SELECT id as \"id: crate::ulid::ActiveBlockId\", ...")` direct-typed query for new sqlx call sites that can decode the typed-id column without an intermediate `BlockRow`. Future sessions can pick whichever recipe fits the shape of the helper without inventing a new pattern.
- **Three backlink resolvers shared the same retype shape.** Their ID sets all derive from a common base-set query that filters active rows; the boundary cast site is at the `block_rows` Vec construction in each case. Once the first migration was understood, the other two were mechanical.
- **Specta correctly emits all-typed-row Tauri commands** without forcing frontend updates because `ActiveBlockId = string` is a transparent TypeScript alias. This continues to be the green-light pattern that made M1, M1.5, and M2 all zero-frontend-touch for active-helper migrations.

**Lessons learned (for future sessions):**
- **The `query_as::<_, ActiveBlockRow>` non-macro pattern is the lightest retype** when the SQL is dynamic (built via `format!()`). `tag_query::query::eval_tag_query` showed this — no doc-string column-cast hints needed; sqlx's `FromRow` + `sqlx::Type` transparent handles everything. Use this whenever the call site already uses non-macro `query_as`.
- **The `query_as!` column-cast hint pattern is the right retype** when the SQL is a literal in the macro (sqlx's compile-time validation needs the literal). `pagination::list_backlinks` showed this. The hint is `id as "id: crate::ulid::ActiveBlockId"` with the fully-qualified path.
- **The boundary-cast pattern is the right retype** when the helper's caller pre-filters active IDs via a separate query (the helper itself doesn't run the active filter). The three backlink resolvers all do this — they pre-filter `base_ids` via one query, then `fetch_block_rows_by_ids` for full row data via a different query that doesn't re-filter. The cast records the trust transition in the type system.
- **Architectural decisions belong before the code, not after.** The dispatcher refactor decision was correctly deferred to M3 because (per AGENTS.md "Architectural Stability") it changes the IPC surface; deferring forces an explicit "split-IPC vs. narrow-at-callsite" decision rather than a quiet code-driven outcome.

**Commit plan:** single commit, not pushed.

## Session 619 — MAINT-113 M1.5 closed: `ActiveBlockRow` parallel struct + 2 helpers retyped (2026-05-02)

| Metadata | Value |
|----------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-02 |
| **Subagents** | orchestrator-only (refactor with cross-file dependencies; per PROMPT.md, kitchen-sink refactors are run orchestrator-direct) |
| **Items closed** | — (MAINT-113 M1.5 closed; MAINT-113 remains open for M2) |
| **Items modified** | MAINT-113 (M1.5 progress recorded in summary + detail; M2 plan refined with the `list_blocks_inner` dispatcher decision) |
| **Tests added** | +4 backend (`pagination::tests::active_row_conversions::*`: byte-identity for `From<ActiveBlockRow> for BlockRow` and `From<ActiveProjectedAgendaEntry>`; uppercase normalisation + field preservation for `from_block_row_unchecked`). Plus 5 test-site `.into()` updates that don't change coverage. |
| **Files touched** | 9 source + REVIEW-LATER + SESSION-LOG |

**Summary:** Closed MAINT-113 M1.5 — the parallel-types path for the row-type retype that M1 deferred. Added `ActiveBlockRow` and `ActiveProjectedAgendaEntry` mirror structs to `pagination/mod.rs` with always-safe `From<Active*> for *Raw*` downcasts and a boundary-cast helper. Retyped two of the three remaining M1 helpers — `fts::search_fts` + `commands::queries::search_blocks_inner` + the `search_blocks` Tauri command (`PageResponse<ActiveBlockRow>`); `commands::agenda::list_projected_agenda_inner` + `list_projected_agenda_on_the_fly` + the `list_projected_agenda` Tauri command (`PageResponse<ActiveProjectedAgendaEntry>`). The third helper, `list_children`, deferred to M2 because it's a leaf of the polymorphic `list_blocks_inner` dispatcher that also fans into `list_trash` (deleted blocks); the dispatcher decision (split-off-trash vs. narrow-at-callsite) belongs in M2 before any code lands.

**Design decision:** Parallel-types path chosen over the `BlockRow<Id = String>` generic that was experimented in M1. The generic ran into two `specta-typescript` 0.0.11 limitations (no Rust generic-default emit; `specta::Type` derive's `PLACEHOLDER_Id` codegen drops `Id: Clone` bounds through embedded structs). The parallel struct duplicates 13 fields but is structurally clean: `ActiveBlockRow` is a strict subset of `BlockRow`, with always-safe `From<ActiveBlockRow> for BlockRow`, and at the wire level both have `id: string` (TS structural typing accepts each in place of the other).

**REVIEW-LATER impact:**
- **Top-level open count:** 114 → 114 (no closure; MAINT-113 remains open with M1.5 closed and M2 next).
- **Detail entries:** 149 → 149 (MAINT-113's milestone block rewritten in place; no add/remove).
- **Previously resolved:** 947+ (unchanged).

**Files touched (this session):**
- `src-tauri/src/pagination/mod.rs` (+99 LOC; `ActiveBlockRow` + `ActiveProjectedAgendaEntry` structs, `From` conversions, doc rewrite explaining the parallel-types decision)
- `src-tauri/src/fts/search.rs` (return type; boundary cast at the FtsSearchRow → ActiveBlockRow construction; import update)
- `src-tauri/src/commands/queries.rs` (`search_blocks_inner` and `search_blocks` Tauri command return type; import update)
- `src-tauri/src/commands/agenda.rs` (`list_projected_agenda_inner` + `list_projected_agenda_on_the_fly` + `list_projected_agenda` return types; cache-path and on-the-fly-path entry construction; 2 cursor encode `.into()` widenings; import update)
- `src-tauri/src/commands/mod.rs` (`RepeatingBlockRow::to_block_row()` → `to_active_block_row()`)
- `src-tauri/src/gcal_push/connector.rs` (boundary downcast: `entries: Vec<ProjectedAgendaEntry>` via `.into_iter().map(Into::into).collect()` since the digest pipeline only consumes row content)
- `src-tauri/src/fts/tests.rs` (3 sites: 2 `all_ids.push(item.id.clone().into())` + 1 `Vec<&str>` rebuild from `id.as_str()`)
- `src-tauri/src/mcp/tools_ro/tests.rs` (1 site: `b.id.clone().into()` for `Vec<String>` collection)
- `src-tauri/src/cache/tests.rs` (1 site: `e.block.id.clone().into()` for tuple collection)
- `src-tauri/src/commands/tests/agenda_cmd_tests.rs` (1 site: `entry.block.id.clone().into()` for tuple collection)
- `src-tauri/src/pagination/tests.rs` (+143 LOC; 4 new `active_row_conversions::*` unit tests pinning the conversion contracts)
- `src/lib/bindings.ts` (regenerated: `ActiveBlockRow` + `ActiveProjectedAgendaEntry` types added; `searchBlocks` and `listProjectedAgenda` return types updated; `ProjectedAgendaEntry` no longer emitted as no command references it directly)
- `REVIEW-LATER.md` (MAINT-113 entry — summary row + detail block rewritten with M1.5 closed, M2 plan refined)
- `SESSION-LOG.md` (this entry + Recent milestones bump)

**Verification:**
- `cd src-tauri && cargo nextest run` — 3429 tests run, 3429 passed (3 skipped).
- `npx vitest run` — 9078 frontend tests pass.
- `cargo test -- specta_tests --ignored` — bindings regenerated; `ts_bindings_up_to_date` passes.
- `npx tsc --noEmit` — 0 errors (frontend types fully accept the new bindings without changes).
- `prek run --all-files` — pending until commit.

**Process notes:**
- **The parallel-types path was the correct call.** The M1 generic experiment surfaced specta-typescript constraints that would have required either a frontend-wide rename (~69 files) or an upstream `specta-typescript` fix. Parallel structs give the same type-safety win at the producer signature with zero frontend ripple — the `ActiveBlockId = string` alias means `ActiveBlockRow` and `BlockRow` are TS-structurally compatible, so frontend code that explicitly types `BlockRow` continues to compile.
- **Dispatcher refactors are M2-scope.** The `list_children` migration deferred to M2 because `list_blocks_inner` fans into both active and deleted-block paths. Migrating any one leaf forces the dispatcher to either split or narrow. PROMPT.md "Architectural Stability" warns against significant architectural changes without explicit user approval; the dispatcher split is exactly that kind of change.
- **Boundary downcasts make trust transitions explicit.** `gcal_push::connector` consumes `ActiveProjectedAgendaEntry` from the agenda helper but feeds the digest pipeline that only cares about row content. The explicit `.into_iter().map(Into::into).collect()` downcast at the boundary is a documented trust transition rather than a hidden coercion — exactly the kind of code-review surface MAINT-113 wants to expose.

**Lessons learned (for future sessions):**
- **`From<Active*> for *Raw*` is the right asymmetry.** Active is a strict subset of Raw, so the always-safe direction is downcast (Active → Raw). The unsafe direction (`from_block_row_unchecked`) is documented as boundary-only. This matches the `BlockId` / `ActiveBlockId` asymmetry and is consistent throughout the codebase.
- **TS structural typing with type-aliased newtypes is a free lunch.** `ActiveBlockId = string` means every Rust newtype that contains an `ActiveBlockId` is structurally compatible with the same struct shape using `string`. No frontend code changes required. This pattern should be the default for any future "tagged-but-wire-equivalent" newtype.
- **Specta drops emitted types that aren't reachable from a command return type.** `ProjectedAgendaEntry` was removed from `bindings.ts` because no command returns it directly anymore. The frontend `src/lib/tauri.ts` keeps a manually-defined `ProjectedAgendaEntry` interface, which TS structural-types cleanly against the new `ActiveProjectedAgendaEntry`. No frontend update needed.

**Commit plan:** single commit, not pushed.

## Session 618 — MAINT-113 M1 partial: `ActiveBlockId` newtype + `verify_active` gate (2026-05-02)

| Metadata | Value |
|----------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-02 |
| **Subagents** | orchestrator-only (refactor with cross-file dependencies; per PROMPT.md "Common Pitfalls", kitchen-sink refactors > 10 consumer call sites are run orchestrator-direct rather than handed to a single subagent) |
| **Items closed** | — (MAINT-113 M1 partially closed; remains open with M1.5/M2/M3 plan) |
| **Items modified** | MAINT-113 (M1 progress recorded in summary + detail) |
| **Tests added** | +13 backend (7 type-level for `ActiveBlockId`, 6 DB-backed for `verify_active`) |
| **Files touched** | 6 source + REVIEW-LATER + SESSION-LOG |

**Summary:** Landed the foundation of MAINT-113 (lifting AGENTS.md invariant #9 into the Rust type system): introduced an `ActiveBlockId(String)` newtype + a `verify_active(pool, &BlockId) -> Result<ActiveBlockId>` SQL gate that checks `is_conflict = 0 AND deleted_at IS NULL`. Retyped `PageLink.source_id` / `target_id` to `ActiveBlockId` as the first concrete demonstration of the design (1 of the 4 helpers spec'd for M1). Removed `soft_delete::get_descendants` — dead code with zero production callers. Specta emits `ActiveBlockId` as a transparent `string` alias (`export type ActiveBlockId = string;`), keeping every existing TypeScript consumer compatible. The remaining three helpers (`list_children`, `list_projected_agenda_inner`, `search_blocks_inner`) are deferred to M1.5/M2 because the natural migration target — `BlockRow<Id = String>` — runs into two friction points with `specta-typescript` 0.0.11 (does not emit Rust generic defaults; `specta::Type` derive's `PLACEHOLDER_Id` codegen doesn't propagate `Id: Clone` bounds through embedded generic structs). Both findings documented in code (`pagination/mod.rs` header comment) and in REVIEW-LATER (with the M1.5 plan).

**Design decision (spec-literal vs reframed):** User picked spec-literal M1 over a reframed thin slice. The spec's 3-4h estimate undercounted the row-type retype cascade (28+ test-site touches per helper plus a frontend ripple) — Stage A (newtype + gate + tests + PageLink retype + dead-code drop) shipped in this session as the defensible foundation; the BlockRow generic experiment was tried, failed on `specta-typescript` limitations, and rolled back with the rationale captured inline.

**REVIEW-LATER impact:**
- **Top-level open count:** 114 → 114 (no closure; MAINT-113 stays open with M1 partial-progress note).
- **Detail entries:** 149 → 149 (MAINT-113's milestone plan rewritten in place; no add/remove).
- **Previously resolved:** 947+ (unchanged).

**Files touched (this session):**
- `src-tauri/src/ulid.rs` (+196 LOC; `ActiveBlockId` newtype + `verify_active` + impls)
- `src-tauri/src/ulid/tests.rs` (+201 LOC; 13 unit tests for the newtype + gate)
- `src-tauri/src/commands/compaction.rs` (`PageLink.source_id` + `target_id` typed to `ActiveBlockId`; 4-line doc note)
- `src-tauri/src/soft_delete/mod.rs` (−25 LOC; `get_descendants` removed + 2 test fns; replaced with a 12-line removal-rationale comment)
- `src-tauri/src/block_descendants.rs` (doc table updated: 4 inline-CTE sites → 3, with a note about the `get_descendants` removal)
- `src-tauri/src/commands/tests/query_cmd_tests.rs` (2 sites: `.into()` on `link.source_id` / `link.target_id` to widen back to `String` for an existing `HashSet<(String, String)>` collection target)
- `src/lib/bindings.ts` (+1 type alias `ActiveBlockId = string`; `PageLink.source_id` / `target_id` retyped; trailing-whitespace formatter noise on doc-comment lines unrelated to this change)
- `REVIEW-LATER.md` (MAINT-113 entry rewritten — summary row + detail block; "Last updated" line bumped to Session 618)
- `SESSION-LOG.md` (this entry)

**Verification:**
- `cd src-tauri && cargo nextest run` — 3429 tests run, 3429 passed (3 skipped). Includes the 13 new tests and the `specta_tests::ts_bindings_up_to_date` check.
- `npx vitest run` — 9078 frontend tests pass (1 flake unrelated to this change: `SearchPanel > navigates to parent page when clicking a result with parent_id` — passes in isolation).
- `cargo test -- specta_tests --ignored` — bindings regenerated; no semantic-change beyond the new `ActiveBlockId` alias and `PageLink` retype.
- `prek run --all-files` — pending until commit.

**Process notes:**
- **Orchestrator-direct beats a single subagent for type-system refactors.** The change touches 6 source files in non-trivial ways and depends on a tight feedback loop with the Rust compiler, sqlx's compile-time query macro, and specta's derive macro. A subagent would have stalled on the `Id: Clone` derive failure (which only surfaces when actually trying to embed `BlockRow<Id>` inside `ProjectedAgendaEntry<Id>`). Per PROMPT.md "Common Pitfalls", refactors that touch >10 consumer call sites or require coordinated edits across many test fixtures should be run orchestrator-direct.
- **Spec-literal commitment + honest scoping ≠ contradiction.** The user picked the spec-literal M1 framing. When the row-type retype hit the `specta-typescript` limitation, the orchestrator-direct path was: experiment, observe the failure mode, capture the finding in code + REVIEW-LATER, revert the experiment, lock in the partial close. The spec's 3-4h estimate was wrong; the partial close is the right shipping unit.
- **Specta `serde(transparent)` newtype emission is the green-light pattern.** Specta v2-rc.24 emits `#[serde(transparent)]` newtypes (with `sqlx::Type` + `specta::Type` derives) as TypeScript type aliases (`export type X = string;`). This is the mechanism that keeps the wire format identical and the frontend zero-touch. Future newtypes following the same pattern can be confidently added without frontend ripple.

**Lessons learned (for future sessions):**
- **`specta-typescript` 0.0.11 does NOT emit Rust generic defaults.** `pub struct BlockRow<Id = String>` becomes `BlockRow<Id>` in TS — the default is dropped. Frontend code that imports `BlockRow` bare requires updating to `BlockRow<string>`. This is the blocker for the row-type retype path. M1.5 needs to either pick a `specta-typescript` version that emits defaults (none today) or use a parallel `ActiveBlockRow` struct.
- **`specta::Type` derive macro on a generic struct embedding another generic struct fails when the outer struct's type parameter has a `Clone` bound on the inner.** The macro generates a `PLACEHOLDER_Id` placeholder type that doesn't carry the where-clause forward. Workaround: drop the `Clone` bound on the struct definition and rely on field-level Clone derivation at instantiation sites.
- **Helpers spec'd as "5 high-traffic" should be sized by their direct-caller cost, not their SQL-site count.** `get_descendants` had 0 production callers (dead code), `list_children` has 28 test sites + 1 production caller, `list_projected_agenda_inner` has 22 test sites + 4 production callers. The actual M1 retype cost is dominated by the test fixtures, not the helper signatures.

**Commit plan:** single commit, not pushed.

**Summary:** Closed 3 S-cost frontend cleanups. The 4-into-3 reduction follows the now-established pattern from sessions 613 (MAINT-178), 614 (TEST-6), and 617 (MAINT-183) — each batch sees one subagent canceled by the user, and the orchestrator drops it rather than restarting. MAINT-183's `markdown-serialize.ts` zero-dep cleanup is left for a future session.

MAINT-184 extracted twin module-private `resolveAndInsertBlockLink` and `resolveAndInsertBlockRef` helpers in their respective picker files. Each helper encapsulates the async items lookup, exact-match check, `onCreate` fallback, plain-text fallback, and error-fallback logic that was previously duplicated between the InputRule and command paths. Both call sites now collapse to ~5-line wrappers passing `(editor, text, insertPos, options, errorMessage)`. The `errorMessage` parameter preserves differentiated log strings the existing tests assert on (`'input rule'` vs `'resolveBlockRefFromSelection'`). Net −25 LOC across the 2 picker files. Per the spec, the helpers stayed as twins (one per picker file) rather than being genericized across pickers — that's a separate, larger refactor.

MAINT-185 mirrored the refs-bag pattern from `use-roving-editor.ts:258-289` to `use-block-keyboard.ts`. The original `useCallback(handleKeyDown, [16 deps])` is now `useCallback(handleKeyDown, [editor])` — 16 → 1 deps. The 15 callbacks (plus `isLastBlock`) are stored in a single `useRef<BlockKeyboardCallbacks>` bag assigned during render (consolidating into one bag rather than 15 individual refs as in the reference pattern — a justified deviation since the hook already receives a typed `BlockKeyboardCallbacks` object). Two new tests pin the contract:
- "keydown listener is not re-attached when callback identities change across re-renders" — uses `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` spies on the listener target; counts unchanged across 3 renders with fresh callbacks.
- "latest callback closure is invoked via the refs bag after a re-render" — `escV1` not called, `escV2` called after a re-render — proves the bag picks up the latest closure.

The "stable handler identity" property the refactor establishes is observable through `addEventListener` counts (since the hook's public API returns `void`, not the handler itself). The build subagent's substitution of count-stability for strict `Object.is` is correct — the actual property is "listener doesn't churn", which the count test measures directly.

TEST-FE-8 added `role="alert"` to the `<div className="pairing-error">` error container in `PairingDialog.tsx` (the element previously had only `aria-live="polite"`). Per WAI-ARIA, when both are present `aria-live` takes precedence — so the existing polite announcement behavior is preserved. The test side then converted all 7 `document.querySelector('.pairing-error')` (and `.pairing-error p`) sites to `await screen.findByRole('alert')` + `toHaveTextContent(/regex/i)`, removing 7 `waitFor(...)` wrappers (since `findByRole` already retries internally). Regex assertions like `/Failed to start pairing:.*network error/i` are case-insensitive and resilient to i18n key changes. The reviewer verified no `role="alert"` collision risk (PairingDialog tests render the dialog in isolation; the 12 other `role="alert"` elements in the codebase are not present in the test DOM).

Reviewer verified the deviation from the reference pattern (single bag vs 15 individual refs) is justified by the hook's already-typed `BlockKeyboardCallbacks` interface.

- **TEST-FE-8** (PASS): `role="alert"` addition is non-behavior-changing (`aria-live` overrides). All 7 sites use `findByRole('alert')` + `toHaveTextContent(/regex/i)`. 43/43 PairingDialog tests pass. Reviewer verified no SR-announcement regression and no collision risk.

The pattern looks intentional from the user's side — possibly cancellations are part of UX testing the orchestrator's recovery, possibly they reflect a slow / stuck subagent.
